========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I guess I have. It’s hard for me to say “depressed” because I keep telling myself I’m just stressed or I’m tired or I’m being dramatic, but if I’m being honest, the low feeling has been there most days for a while now.

It’s probably been around four months, give or take. Like this heavy mood, and feeling guilty about things, and just not really caring about stuff the way I normally would. At first I thought it was just a bad week turning into a bad couple weeks, but it didn’t really lift. And after the trouble I had at work, it got a lot harder to ignore. That’s when it felt less like, “Oh, I’m just in a funk,” and more like, “Okay, something is actually off here.”

Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel like I’m not really myself lately.

I used to be pretty into my weekends, like I’d play soccer with some guys, and even if I was tired from the week I’d usually still want to go because once I got there it felt good, you know? Now I’ll see the messages about it and just… ignore them, or tell myself I’ll answer later, and then I don’t. It’s not even that I hate it now, it’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to show up.

Same with cooking. I used to like making dinner for my girlfriend — not fancy chef stuff or anything, but I liked planning something, going to the store, making it feel like a nice night. Lately it feels like this huge task, and the fun part of it is just gone.

Even stuff like checking out small galleries, which used to be something I’d do just because it got me out of my head, I haven’t cared about. And at work, I used to actually enjoy helping the newer sales guys figure things out, like giving them tips or walking them through how to handle certain accounts. Now when they ask me something, I feel bad because I know I should care, but I just don’t have that spark for it. It all feels kind of flat.

Like, some days I’ll get through almost the whole day and realize I basically didn’t eat anything, not because I’m trying to or anything, I just forget meals completely. And then other nights it’s the total opposite — I’m up late, after midnight, and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I can’t stop myself.\n\nIt’s just been weird enough that I started thinking, okay, maybe I should actually talk to somebody, because it doesn’t feel like my normal routine anymore.\n\nDoctor: When you eat like that after midnight, are you waking up from sleep to do it?\n\nPatient: Not really waking up from sleep, no. It’s more like I’m already still up way too late, and then I suddenly realize I barely ate during the day, or I just get this urge to order food or grab whatever snacks are around. Like I’ll go most of the day and completely forget meals, and then after midnight I’m eating takeout or a bunch of random stuff like I can’t slow down. It’s not like I go to bed and then wake up hungry—it’s more that my whole eating schedule is just… messed up.\n\nDoctor: When those late-night eating episodes happen, do you feel like you can’t stop eating once you’ve started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Not every single time, but when it happens it feels like once I start, I’m not really making normal decisions about it. Like I’ll order takeout after midnight or start grabbing snacks, and then it’s just this whole stretch where I’m eating whatever’s there, one thing after another. It’s not even like I’m enjoying it that much the whole time — it’s more like I suddenly realize I barely ate all day, and then I’m tearing through food like my body’s trying to catch up or something.\n\nAnd that’s what freaked me out, honestly, because it’s such a swing from forgetting meals completely during the day to then being up late and eating way more than feels normal. It doesn’t feel like my usual appetite or routine.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight or about the shape of your body?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I’m not thrilled if my clothes feel tighter or something, like anyone, but I’m not sitting there counting calories because I’m scared of gaining weight, and I’m not avoiding food because of how I think I look.\n\nIt’s more like I just genuinely forget to eat or I’m distracted, and then later it catches up with me in this messy way. The late-night eating bothers me because it feels out of control and makes me feel kind of gross afterward, but I wouldn’t say it’s about my body shape exactly. It’s more the pattern that worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. It’s hard for me to say “depressed” because I keep telling myself I’m just stressed or I’m tired or I’m being dramatic, but if I’m being honest, the low feeling has been there most days for a while now.\n\nIt’s probably been around four months, give or take. Like this heavy mood, and feeling guilty about things, and just not really caring about stuff the way I normally would. At first I thought it was just a bad week turning into a bad couple weeks, but it didn’t really lift. And after the trouble I had at work, it got a lot harder to ignore. That’s when it felt less like, “Oh, I’m just in a funk,” and more like, “Okay, something is actually off here.”\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel like I’m not really myself lately.\n\nI used to be pretty into my weekends, like I’d play soccer with some guys, and even if I was tired from the week I’d usually still want to go because once I got there it felt good, you know? Now I’ll see the messages about it and just… ignore them, or tell myself I’ll answer later, and then I don’t. It’s not even that I hate it now, it’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to show up.\n\nSame with cooking. I used to like making dinner for my girlfriend — not fancy chef stuff or anything, but I liked planning something, going to the store, making it feel like a nice night. Yeah, I have. It’s like… even if I technically lie down for a long time, it doesn’t feel like I really get my energy back. I’ll spend hours resting, and then the next day my body still feels drained.

I’ll be thinking, “Why am I this worn out from something I normally just do without thinking?” It’s embarrassing, honestly, because it makes me feel kind of weak or like I’m dragging myself through things that used to be normal.

Not every single time, but when it happens it feels like once I start, I’m not really making normal decisions about it. Like I’ll order takeout after midnight or start grabbing snacks, and then it’s just this whole stretch where I’m eating whatever’s there, one thing after another. It’s not even like I’m enjoying it that much the whole time — it’s more like I suddenly realize I barely ate all day, and then I’m tearing through food like my body’s trying to catch up or something.\n\nAnd that’s what freaked me out, honestly, because it’s such a swing from forgetting meals completely during the day to then being up late and eating way more than feels normal. It doesn’t feel like my usual appetite or routine.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight or about the shape of your body?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I’m not thrilled if my clothes feel tighter or something, like anyone, but I’m not sitting there counting calories because I’m scared of gaining weight, and I’m not avoiding food because of how I think I look.\n\nIt’s more like I just genuinely forget to eat or I’m distracted, and then later it catches up with me in this messy way. The late-night eating bothers me because it feels out of control and makes me feel kind of gross afterward, but I wouldn’t say it’s about my body shape exactly. It’s more the pattern that worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. It’s hard for me to say “depressed” because I keep telling myself I’m just stressed or I’m tired or I’m being dramatic, but if I’m being honest, the low feeling has been there most days for a while now.\n\nIt’s probably been around four months, give or take. Like this heavy mood, and feeling guilty about things, and just not really caring about stuff the way I normally would. At first I thought it was just a bad week turning into a bad couple weeks, but it didn’t really lift. And after the trouble I had at work, it got a lot harder to ignore. That’s when it felt less like, “Oh, I’m just in a funk,” and more like, “Okay, something is actually off here.”\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel like I’m not really myself lately.\n\nI used to be pretty into my weekends, like I’d play soccer with some guys, and even if I was tired from the week I’d usually still want to go because once I got there it felt good, you know? Now I’ll see the messages about it and just… ignore them, or tell myself I’ll answer later, and then I don’t. It’s not even that I hate it now, it’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to show up.\n\nSame with cooking. I used to like making dinner for my girlfriend — not fancy chef stuff or anything, but I liked planning something, going to the store, making it feel like a nice night. Lately it feels like this huge task, and the fun part of it is just gone.\n\nEven stuff like checking out small galleries, which used to be something I’d do just because it got me out of my head, I haven’t cared about. And at work, I used to actually enjoy helping the newer sales guys figure things out, like giving them tips or walking them through how to handle certain accounts. Now when they ask me something, I feel bad because I know I should care, but I just don’t have that spark for it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It’s like… even if I technically lie down for a long time, it doesn’t feel like I really get my energy back. I’ll spend hours resting, and then the next day my body still feels drained.\n\nAnd it’s not just “I don’t feel like doing stuff.” It’s physical, too. Like at work, carrying sample cases into a client meeting will feel way harder than it should. I’ll be thinking, “Why am I this worn out from something I normally just do without thinking?” It’s embarrassing, honestly, because it makes me feel kind of weak or like I’m dragging myself through things that used to be normal.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah… my sleep’s been pretty messed up. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get in bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel tired, but then my brain just won’t shut off. I’ll start running through work stuff — pitches, what I should’ve said to a client, what I need to say next time, different ways to phrase things — and it just keeps looping. It’s like I’m rehearsing conversations I’m not even having yet.

Or I’ll fall asleep for a bit, but then I wake up way before my alarm, like way too early, and I’m just… awake. Not rested, though. It’s weird because I know I barely slept, and part of me is like, “You need to go back to sleep,” but my body feels kind of wired, like I’m already switched on. So then I just lie there, thinking about the day before it’s even started. It’s been happening a lot, most nights honestly.

It’s more like I’m already still up way too late, and then I suddenly realize I barely ate during the day, or I just get this urge to order food or grab whatever snacks are around. It’s not like I go to bed and then wake up hungry—it’s more that my whole eating schedule is just… messed up.\n\nDoctor: When those late-night eating episodes happen, do you feel like you can’t stop eating once you’ve started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Not every single time, but when it happens it feels like once I start, I’m not really making normal decisions about it. Like I’ll order takeout after midnight or start grabbing snacks, and then it’s just this whole stretch where I’m eating whatever’s there, one thing after another. It’s not even like I’m enjoying it that much the whole time — it’s more like I suddenly realize I barely ate all day, and then I’m tearing through food like my body’s trying to catch up or something.\n\nAnd that’s what freaked me out, honestly, because it’s such a swing from forgetting meals completely during the day to then being up late and eating way more than feels normal. It doesn’t feel like my usual appetite or routine.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight or about the shape of your body?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I’m not thrilled if my clothes feel tighter or something, like anyone, but I’m not sitting there counting calories because I’m scared of gaining weight, and I’m not avoiding food because of how I think I look.\n\nIt’s more like I just genuinely forget to eat or I’m distracted, and then later it catches up with me in this messy way. The late-night eating bothers me because it feels out of control and makes me feel kind of gross afterward, but I wouldn’t say it’s about my body shape exactly. It’s more the pattern that worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. It’s hard for me to say “depressed” because I keep telling myself I’m just stressed or I’m tired or I’m being dramatic, but if I’m being honest, the low feeling has been there most days for a while now.\n\nIt’s probably been around four months, give or take. Like this heavy mood, and feeling guilty about things, and just not really caring about stuff the way I normally would. At first I thought it was just a bad week turning into a bad couple weeks, but it didn’t really lift. And after the trouble I had at work, it got a lot harder to ignore. That’s when it felt less like, “Oh, I’m just in a funk,” and more like, “Okay, something is actually off here.”\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel like I’m not really myself lately.\n\nI used to be pretty into my weekends, like I’d play soccer with some guys, and even if I was tired from the week I’d usually still want to go because once I got there it felt good, you know? Now I’ll see the messages about it and just… ignore them, or tell myself I’ll answer later, and then I don’t. It’s not even that I hate it now, it’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to show up.\n\nSame with cooking. I used to like making dinner for my girlfriend — not fancy chef stuff or anything, but I liked planning something, going to the store, making it feel like a nice night. Lately it feels like this huge task, and the fun part of it is just gone.\n\nEven stuff like checking out small galleries, which used to be something I’d do just because it got me out of my head, I haven’t cared about. And at work, I used to actually enjoy helping the newer sales guys figure things out, like giving them tips or walking them through how to handle certain accounts. Now when they ask me something, I feel bad because I know I should care, but I just don’t have that spark for it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It’s like… even if I technically lie down for a long time, it doesn’t feel like I really get my energy back. I’ll spend hours resting, and then the next day my body still feels drained.\n\nAnd it’s not just “I don’t feel like doing stuff.” It’s physical, too. Like at work, carrying sample cases into a client meeting will feel way harder than it should. I’ll be thinking, “Why am I this worn out from something I normally just do without thinking?” It’s embarrassing, honestly, because it makes me feel kind of weak or like I’m dragging myself through things that used to be normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my sleep’s been pretty messed up. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get in bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel tired, but then my brain just won’t shut off. I’ll start running through work stuff — pitches, what I should’ve said to a client, what I need to say next time, different ways to phrase things — and it just keeps looping. It’s like I’m rehearsing conversations I’m not even having yet.\n\nOr I’ll fall asleep for a bit, but then I wake up way before my alarm, like way too early, and I’m just… awake. Not rested, though. It’s weird because I know I barely slept, and part of me is like, “You need to go back to sleep,” but my body feels kind of wired, like I’m already switched on. So then I just lie there, thinking about the day before it’s even started. It’s been happening a lot, most nights honestly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Yeah… I mean, not like I’ve sat down and made some plan or anything like that. I haven’t done that. But I’d be lying if I said the thoughts weren’t there sometimes.

It’s more like… I’ll be so tired of feeling like this that I’ll think, “I wish I could just not wake up for a while.” Not even necessarily die, exactly, but just stop having to be conscious and deal with everything for a bit. Like if I could press pause on myself and not have to feel anything, that sounds… honestly kind of relieving sometimes.

But no, I haven’t planned to hurt myself. I haven’t picked a way or anything. It’s more those thoughts of not wanting to be here in the moment, or wanting everything to just go quiet.

The late-night eating bothers me because it feels out of control and makes me feel kind of gross afterward, but I wouldn’t say it’s about my body shape exactly. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the more frustrating things, because my job is a lot of little details, and normally I’m pretty good at keeping track of them.

Like I’ll be entering an order, and I know what I’m supposed to be doing — customer name, quantities, item numbers, shipping details, all that basic stuff — but then my phone buzzes, or a truck goes by outside, or someone just walks past the office, and it completely pulls me off. Not just for a second, either. It’s like my attention gets yanked over there, and then when I look back at the screen I’ve lost the thread of what I was doing.

And then I’ll miss things I normally wouldn’t miss. Basic details. Stuff where afterward I’m like, “How did I not catch that?” It makes me feel kind of stupid, honestly, because these aren’t complicated tasks for me. I’ve done them a million times. But lately it feels like my brain is way too easy to knock off track.

What brings you in?\n\nPatient: Honestly, the thing that kind of scared me into coming in is my eating. It’s been really all over the place lately. Like, some days I’ll get through almost the whole day and realize I basically didn’t eat anything, not because I’m trying to or anything, I just forget meals completely. And then other nights it’s the total opposite — I’m up late, after midnight, and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I can’t stop myself.\n\nIt’s just been weird enough that I started thinking, okay, maybe I should actually talk to somebody, because it doesn’t feel like my normal routine anymore.\n\nDoctor: When you eat like that after midnight, are you waking up from sleep to do it?\n\nPatient: Not really waking up from sleep, no. It’s more like I’m already still up way too late, and then I suddenly realize I barely ate during the day, or I just get this urge to order food or grab whatever snacks are around. Like I’ll go most of the day and completely forget meals, and then after midnight I’m eating takeout or a bunch of random stuff like I can’t slow down. It’s not like I go to bed and then wake up hungry—it’s more that my whole eating schedule is just… messed up.\n\nDoctor: When those late-night eating episodes happen, do you feel like you can’t stop eating once you’ve started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Not every single time, but when it happens it feels like once I start, I’m not really making normal decisions about it. Like I’ll order takeout after midnight or start grabbing snacks, and then it’s just this whole stretch where I’m eating whatever’s there, one thing after another. It’s not even like I’m enjoying it that much the whole time — it’s more like I suddenly realize I barely ate all day, and then I’m tearing through food like my body’s trying to catch up or something.\n\nAnd that’s what freaked me out, honestly, because it’s such a swing from forgetting meals completely during the day to then being up late and eating way more than feels normal. It doesn’t feel like my usual appetite or routine.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight or about the shape of your body?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I’m not thrilled if my clothes feel tighter or something, like anyone, but I’m not sitting there counting calories because I’m scared of gaining weight, and I’m not avoiding food because of how I think I look.\n\nIt’s more like I just genuinely forget to eat or I’m distracted, and then later it catches up with me in this messy way. The late-night eating bothers me because it feels out of control and makes me feel kind of gross afterward, but I wouldn’t say it’s about my body shape exactly. It’s more the pattern that worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. It’s hard for me to say “depressed” because I keep telling myself I’m just stressed or I’m tired or I’m being dramatic, but if I’m being honest, the low feeling has been there most days for a while now.\n\nIt’s probably been around four months, give or take. Like this heavy mood, and feeling guilty about things, and just not really caring about stuff the way I normally would. At first I thought it was just a bad week turning into a bad couple weeks, but it didn’t really lift. And after the trouble I had at work, it got a lot harder to ignore. That’s when it felt less like, “Oh, I’m just in a funk,” and more like, “Okay, something is actually off here.”\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel like I’m not really myself lately.\n\nI used to be pretty into my weekends, like I’d play soccer with some guys, and even if I was tired from the week I’d usually still want to go because once I got there it felt good, you know? Now I’ll see the messages about it and just… ignore them, or tell myself I’ll answer later, and then I don’t. It’s not even that I hate it now, it’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to show up.\n\nSame with cooking. I used to like making dinner for my girlfriend — not fancy chef stuff or anything, but I liked planning something, going to the store, making it feel like a nice night. Lately it feels like this huge task, and the fun part of it is just gone.\n\nEven stuff like checking out small galleries, which used to be something I’d do just because it got me out of my head, I haven’t cared about. And at work, I used to actually enjoy helping the newer sales guys figure things out, like giving them tips or walking them through how to handle certain accounts. Now when they ask me something, I feel bad because I know I should care, but I just don’t have that spark for it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It’s like… even if I technically lie down for a long time, it doesn’t feel like I really get my energy back. I’ll spend hours resting, and then the next day my body still feels drained.\n\nAnd it’s not just “I don’t feel like doing stuff.” It’s physical, too. Like at work, carrying sample cases into a client meeting will feel way harder than it should. I’ll be thinking, “Why am I this worn out from something I normally just do without thinking?” It’s embarrassing, honestly, because it makes me feel kind of weak or like I’m dragging myself through things that used to be normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my sleep’s been pretty messed up. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get in bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel tired, but then my brain just won’t shut off. I’ll start running through work stuff — pitches, what I should’ve said to a client, what I need to say next time, different ways to phrase things — and it just keeps looping. It’s like I’m rehearsing conversations I’m not even having yet.\n\nOr I’ll fall asleep for a bit, but then I wake up way before my alarm, like way too early, and I’m just… awake. Not rested, though. It’s weird because I know I barely slept, and part of me is like, “You need to go back to sleep,” but my body feels kind of wired, like I’m already switched on. So then I just lie there, thinking about the day before it’s even started. It’s been happening a lot, most nights honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like I’ve sat down and made some plan or anything like that. I haven’t done that. But I’d be lying if I said the thoughts weren’t there sometimes.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be so tired of feeling like this that I’ll think, “I wish I could just not wake up for a while.” Not even necessarily die, exactly, but just stop having to be conscious and deal with everything for a bit. Like if I could press pause on myself and not have to feel anything, that sounds… honestly kind of relieving sometimes.\n\nBut no, I haven’t planned to hurt myself. I haven’t picked a way or anything. It’s more those thoughts of not wanting to be here in the moment, or wanting everything to just go quiet.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Not just for a second, either. It’s like my attention gets yanked over there, and then when I look back at the screen I’ve lost the thread of what I was doing.\n\nAnd then I’ll miss things I normally wouldn’t miss. Basic details. It’s more those thoughts of not wanting to be here in the moment, or wanting everything to just go quiet.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the more frustrating things, because my job is a lot of little details, and normally I’m pretty good at keeping track of them.\n\nLike I’ll be entering an order, and I know what I’m supposed to be doing — customer name, quantities, item numbers, shipping details, all that basic stuff — but then my phone buzzes, or a truck goes by outside, or someone just walks past the office, and it completely pulls me off. Not just for a second, either. It’s like my attention gets yanked over there, and then when I look back at the screen I’ve lost the thread of what I was doing.\n\nAnd then I’ll miss things I normally wouldn’t miss. Basic details. Stuff where afterward I’m like, “How did I not catch that?” It makes me feel kind of stupid, honestly, because these aren’t complicated tasks for me. I’ve done them a million times. But lately it feels like my brain is way too easy to knock off track.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had less appetite lately?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… kind of, but it’s not just less appetite exactly. It’s been really uneven, and honestly that’s part of what scared me into coming in.

Some days I’ll realize it’s like mid-afternoon and I haven’t eaten anything real at all. Not because I’m trying not to eat, I just… forget, or nothing sounds good, or I’m busy and then suddenly hours are gone. And then other nights it swings the other way where it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I’m starving, just eating whatever’s there.

So I don’t know if I’d call it just “less appetite,” because sometimes it’s like I have no appetite, and then later it’s like I’m making up for the whole day all at once. It feels out of control in a weird way.

What brings you in?\n\nPatient: Honestly, the thing that kind of scared me into coming in is my eating. It’s been really all over the place lately. Like, some days I’ll get through almost the whole day and realize I basically didn’t eat anything, not because I’m trying to or anything, I just forget meals completely. And then other nights it’s the total opposite — I’m up late, after midnight, and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I can’t stop myself.\n\nIt’s just been weird enough that I started thinking, okay, maybe I should actually talk to somebody, because it doesn’t feel like my normal routine anymore.\n\nDoctor: When you eat like that after midnight, are you waking up from sleep to do it?\n\nPatient: Not really waking up from sleep, no. It’s more like I’m already still up way too late, and then I suddenly realize I barely ate during the day, or I just get this urge to order food or grab whatever snacks are around. Like I’ll go most of the day and completely forget meals, and then after midnight I’m eating takeout or a bunch of random stuff like I can’t slow down. It’s not like I go to bed and then wake up hungry—it’s more that my whole eating schedule is just… messed up.\n\nDoctor: When those late-night eating episodes happen, do you feel like you can’t stop eating once you’ve started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Not every single time, but when it happens it feels like once I start, I’m not really making normal decisions about it. Like I’ll order takeout after midnight or start grabbing snacks, and then it’s just this whole stretch where I’m eating whatever’s there, one thing after another. It’s not even like I’m enjoying it that much the whole time — it’s more like I suddenly realize I barely ate all day, and then I’m tearing through food like my body’s trying to catch up or something.\n\nAnd that’s what freaked me out, honestly, because it’s such a swing from forgetting meals completely during the day to then being up late and eating way more than feels normal. It doesn’t feel like my usual appetite or routine.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight or about the shape of your body?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I’m not thrilled if my clothes feel tighter or something, like anyone, but I’m not sitting there counting calories because I’m scared of gaining weight, and I’m not avoiding food because of how I think I look.\n\nIt’s more like I just genuinely forget to eat or I’m distracted, and then later it catches up with me in this messy way. The late-night eating bothers me because it feels out of control and makes me feel kind of gross afterward, but I wouldn’t say it’s about my body shape exactly. It’s more the pattern that worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. It’s hard for me to say “depressed” because I keep telling myself I’m just stressed or I’m tired or I’m being dramatic, but if I’m being honest, the low feeling has been there most days for a while now.\n\nIt’s probably been around four months, give or take. Like this heavy mood, and feeling guilty about things, and just not really caring about stuff the way I normally would. At first I thought it was just a bad week turning into a bad couple weeks, but it didn’t really lift. And after the trouble I had at work, it got a lot harder to ignore. That’s when it felt less like, “Oh, I’m just in a funk,” and more like, “Okay, something is actually off here.”\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel like I’m not really myself lately.\n\nI used to be pretty into my weekends, like I’d play soccer with some guys, and even if I was tired from the week I’d usually still want to go because once I got there it felt good, you know? Now I’ll see the messages about it and just… ignore them, or tell myself I’ll answer later, and then I don’t. It’s not even that I hate it now, it’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to show up.\n\nSame with cooking. I used to like making dinner for my girlfriend — not fancy chef stuff or anything, but I liked planning something, going to the store, making it feel like a nice night. Lately it feels like this huge task, and the fun part of it is just gone.\n\nEven stuff like checking out small galleries, which used to be something I’d do just because it got me out of my head, I haven’t cared about. And at work, I used to actually enjoy helping the newer sales guys figure things out, like giving them tips or walking them through how to handle certain accounts. Now when they ask me something, I feel bad because I know I should care, but I just don’t have that spark for it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It’s like… even if I technically lie down for a long time, it doesn’t feel like I really get my energy back. I’ll spend hours resting, and then the next day my body still feels drained.\n\nAnd it’s not just “I don’t feel like doing stuff.” It’s physical, too. Like at work, carrying sample cases into a client meeting will feel way harder than it should. I’ll be thinking, “Why am I this worn out from something I normally just do without thinking?” It’s embarrassing, honestly, because it makes me feel kind of weak or like I’m dragging myself through things that used to be normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my sleep’s been pretty messed up. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get in bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel tired, but then my brain just won’t shut off. I’ll start running through work stuff — pitches, what I should’ve said to a client, what I need to say next time, different ways to phrase things — and it just keeps looping. It’s like I’m rehearsing conversations I’m not even having yet.\n\nOr I’ll fall asleep for a bit, but then I wake up way before my alarm, like way too early, and I’m just… awake. Not rested, though. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if people would say “slower,” exactly. I haven’t had someone sit me down and say, like, “You’re moving slow” or anything.

It’s more like I feel kind of all over the place depending on where I am. At work, before appointments, I’ll catch myself pacing around my car in the parking lot instead of just going in. Like I’ll get there early, and instead of sitting still I’m walking around the car, checking my phone, tapping my hands on the steering wheel, almost like I can’t settle my body down. I probably look ridiculous if anyone sees me.

But then when I get home, it’s the opposite. I’ll sit on the couch and it feels like getting up to do one basic thing takes forever. Like I know I should stand up, or go get something, or move to the next room, but my body feels heavy and I just sit there thinking about doing it. So I don’t know if it’s that I’m moving slower all the time, but there are definitely times where I feel slowed down, and other times where I’m kind of restless and can’t stop moving.

It’s more like I’m already still up way too late, and then I suddenly realize I barely ate during the day, or I just get this urge to order food or grab whatever snacks are around. Like I’ll go most of the day and completely forget meals, and then after midnight I’m eating takeout or a bunch of random stuff like I can’t slow down. It’s not like I go to bed and then wake up hungry—it’s more that my whole eating schedule is just… messed up.\n\nDoctor: When those late-night eating episodes happen, do you feel like you can’t stop eating once you’ve started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Not every single time, but when it happens it feels like once I start, I’m not really making normal decisions about it. Like I’ll order takeout after midnight or start grabbing snacks, and then it’s just this whole stretch where I’m eating whatever’s there, one thing after another. It’s not even like I’m enjoying it that much the whole time — it’s more like I suddenly realize I barely ate all day, and then I’m tearing through food like my body’s trying to catch up or something.\n\nAnd that’s what freaked me out, honestly, because it’s such a swing from forgetting meals completely during the day to then being up late and eating way more than feels normal. It doesn’t feel like my usual appetite or routine.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight or about the shape of your body?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I’m not thrilled if my clothes feel tighter or something, like anyone, but I’m not sitting there counting calories because I’m scared of gaining weight, and I’m not avoiding food because of how I think I look.\n\nIt’s more like I just genuinely forget to eat or I’m distracted, and then later it catches up with me in this messy way. The late-night eating bothers me because it feels out of control and makes me feel kind of gross afterward, but I wouldn’t say it’s about my body shape exactly. It’s more the pattern that worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. It’s hard for me to say “depressed” because I keep telling myself I’m just stressed or I’m tired or I’m being dramatic, but if I’m being honest, the low feeling has been there most days for a while now.\n\nIt’s probably been around four months, give or take. Like this heavy mood, and feeling guilty about things, and just not really caring about stuff the way I normally would. At first I thought it was just a bad week turning into a bad couple weeks, but it didn’t really lift. And after the trouble I had at work, it got a lot harder to ignore. That’s when it felt less like, “Oh, I’m just in a funk,” and more like, “Okay, something is actually off here.”\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel like I’m not really myself lately.\n\nI used to be pretty into my weekends, like I’d play soccer with some guys, and even if I was tired from the week I’d usually still want to go because once I got there it felt good, you know? Now I’ll see the messages about it and just… ignore them, or tell myself I’ll answer later, and then I don’t. It’s not even that I hate it now, it’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to show up.\n\nSame with cooking. I used to like making dinner for my girlfriend — not fancy chef stuff or anything, but I liked planning something, going to the store, making it feel like a nice night. Lately it feels like this huge task, and the fun part of it is just gone.\n\nEven stuff like checking out small galleries, which used to be something I’d do just because it got me out of my head, I haven’t cared about. And at work, I used to actually enjoy helping the newer sales guys figure things out, like giving them tips or walking them through how to handle certain accounts. Now when they ask me something, I feel bad because I know I should care, but I just don’t have that spark for it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It’s like… even if I technically lie down for a long time, it doesn’t feel like I really get my energy back. I’ll spend hours resting, and then the next day my body still feels drained.\n\nAnd it’s not just “I don’t feel like doing stuff.” It’s physical, too. Like at work, carrying sample cases into a client meeting will feel way harder than it should. I’ll be thinking, “Why am I this worn out from something I normally just do without thinking?” It’s embarrassing, honestly, because it makes me feel kind of weak or like I’m dragging myself through things that used to be normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my sleep’s been pretty messed up. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get in bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel tired, but then my brain just won’t shut off. I’ll start running through work stuff — pitches, what I should’ve said to a client, what I need to say next time, different ways to phrase things — and it just keeps looping. It’s like I’m rehearsing conversations I’m not even having yet.\n\nOr I’ll fall asleep for a bit, but then I wake up way before my alarm, like way too early, and I’m just… awake. Not rested, though. It’s weird because I know I barely slept, and part of me is like, “You need to go back to sleep,” but my body feels kind of wired, like I’m already switched on. So then I just lie there, thinking about the day before it’s even started. It’s been happening a lot, most nights honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like I’ve sat down and made some plan or anything like that. I haven’t done that. But I’d be lying if I said the thoughts weren’t there sometimes.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be so tired of feeling like this that I’ll think, “I wish I could just not wake up for a while.” Not even necessarily die, exactly, but just stop having to be conscious and deal with everything for a bit. Like if I could press pause on myself and not have to feel anything, that sounds… honestly kind of relieving sometimes.\n\nBut no, I haven’t planned to hurt myself. I haven’t picked a way or anything. It’s more those thoughts of not wanting to be here in the moment, or wanting everything to just go quiet.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the more frustrating things, because my job is a lot of little details, and normally I’m pretty good at keeping track of them.\n\nLike I’ll be entering an order, and I know what I’m supposed to be doing — customer name, quantities, item numbers, shipping details, all that basic stuff — but then my phone buzzes, or a truck goes by outside, or someone just walks past the office, and it completely pulls me off. Not just for a second, either. It’s like my attention gets yanked over there, and then when I look back at the screen I’ve lost the thread of what I was doing.\n\nAnd then I’ll miss things I normally wouldn’t miss. Basic details. Stuff where afterward I’m like, “How did I not catch that?” It makes me feel kind of stupid, honestly, because these aren’t complicated tasks for me. I’ve done them a million times. But lately it feels like my brain is way too easy to knock off track.\n\nDoctor: Have you had less appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of, but it’s not just less appetite exactly. It’s been really uneven, and honestly that’s part of what scared me into coming in.\n\nSome days I’ll realize it’s like mid-afternoon and I haven’t eaten anything real at all. Not because I’m trying not to eat, I just… forget, or nothing sounds good, or I’m busy and then suddenly hours are gone. And then other nights it swings the other way where it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I’m starving, just eating whatever’s there.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it just “less appetite,” because sometimes it’s like I have no appetite, and then later it’s like I’m making up for the whole day all at once. It feels out of control in a weird way.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have other people noticed that you’re moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if people would say “slower,” exactly. I haven’t had someone sit me down and say, like, “You’re moving slow” or anything.\n\nIt’s more like I feel kind of all over the place depending on where I am. At work, before appointments, I’ll catch myself pacing around my car in the parking lot instead of just going in. Like I’ll get there early, and instead of sitting still I’m walking around the car, checking my phone, tapping my hands on the steering wheel, almost like I can’t settle my body down. I probably look ridiculous if anyone sees me.\n\nBut then when I get home, it’s the opposite. I’ll sit on the couch and it feels like getting up to do one basic thing takes forever. Like I know I should stand up, or go get something, or move to the next room, but my body feels heavy and I just sit there thinking about doing it. Like I’ll order takeout after midnight or start grabbing snacks, and then it’s just this whole stretch where I’m eating whatever’s there, one thing after another. It’s not even like I’m enjoying it that much the whole time — it’s more like I suddenly realize I barely ate all day, and then I’m tearing through food like my body’s trying to catch up or something.\n\nAnd that’s what freaked me out, honestly, because it’s such a swing from forgetting meals completely during the day to then being up late and eating way more than feels normal. It doesn’t feel like my usual appetite or routine.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight or about the shape of your body?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I’m not thrilled if my clothes feel tighter or something, like anyone, but I’m not sitting there counting calories because I’m scared of gaining weight, and I’m not avoiding food because of how I think I look.\n\nIt’s more like I just genuinely forget to eat or I’m distracted, and then later it catches up with me in this messy way. The late-night eating bothers me because it feels out of control and makes me feel kind of gross afterward, but I wouldn’t say it’s about my body shape exactly. It’s more the pattern that worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. It’s hard for me to say “depressed” because I keep telling myself I’m just stressed or I’m tired or I’m being dramatic, but if I’m being honest, the low feeling has been there most days for a while now.\n\nIt’s probably been around four months, give or take. Like this heavy mood, and feeling guilty about things, and just not really caring about stuff the way I normally would. At first I thought it was just a bad week turning into a bad couple weeks, but it didn’t really lift. And after the trouble I had at work, it got a lot harder to ignore. That’s when it felt less like, “Oh, I’m just in a funk,” and more like, “Okay, something is actually off here.”\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel like I’m not really myself lately.\n\nI used to be pretty into my weekends, like I’d play soccer with some guys, and even if I was tired from the week I’d usually still want to go because once I got there it felt good, you know? Now I’ll see the messages about it and just… ignore them, or tell myself I’ll answer later, and then I don’t. It’s not even that I hate it now, it’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to show up.\n\nSame with cooking. I used to like making dinner for my girlfriend — not fancy chef stuff or anything, but I liked planning something, going to the store, making it feel like a nice night. Lately it feels like this huge task, and the fun part of it is just gone.\n\nEven stuff like checking out small galleries, which used to be something I’d do just because it got me out of my head, I haven’t cared about. And at work, I used to actually enjoy helping the newer sales guys figure things out, like giving them tips or walking them through how to handle certain accounts. Now when they ask me something, I feel bad because I know I should care, but I just don’t have that spark for it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It’s like… even if I technically lie down for a long time, it doesn’t feel like I really get my energy back. I’ll spend hours resting, and then the next day my body still feels drained.\n\nAnd it’s not just “I don’t feel like doing stuff.” It’s physical, too. Like at work, carrying sample cases into a client meeting will feel way harder than it should. I’ll be thinking, “Why am I this worn out from something I normally just do without thinking?” It’s embarrassing, honestly, because it makes me feel kind of weak or like I’m dragging myself through things that used to be normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my sleep’s been pretty messed up. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get in bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel tired, but then my brain just won’t shut off. I’ll start running through work stuff — pitches, what I should’ve said to a client, what I need to say next time, different ways to phrase things — and it just keeps looping. It’s like I’m rehearsing conversations I’m not even having yet.\n\nOr I’ll fall asleep for a bit, but then I wake up way before my alarm, like way too early, and I’m just… awake. Not rested, though. It’s weird because I know I barely slept, and part of me is like, “You need to go back to sleep,” but my body feels kind of wired, like I’m already switched on. So then I just lie there, thinking about the day before it’s even started. It’s been happening a lot, most nights honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like I’ve sat down and made some plan or anything like that. I haven’t done that. But I’d be lying if I said the thoughts weren’t there sometimes.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be so tired of feeling like this that I’ll think, “I wish I could just not wake up for a while.” Not even necessarily die, exactly, but just stop having to be conscious and deal with everything for a bit. Like if I could press pause on myself and not have to feel anything, that sounds… honestly kind of relieving sometimes.\n\nBut no, I haven’t planned to hurt myself. I haven’t picked a way or anything. It’s more those thoughts of not wanting to be here in the moment, or wanting everything to just go quiet.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the more frustrating things, because my job is a lot of little details, and normally I’m pretty good at keeping track of them.\n\nLike I’ll be entering an order, and I know what I’m supposed to be doing — customer name, quantities, item numbers, shipping details, all that basic stuff — but then my phone buzzes, or a truck goes by outside, or someone just walks past the office, and it completely pulls me off. Not just for a second, either. It’s like my attention gets yanked over there, and then when I look back at the screen I’ve lost the thread of what I was doing.\n\nAnd then I’ll miss things I normally wouldn’t miss. Basic details. Stuff where afterward I’m like, “How did I not catch that?” It makes me feel kind of stupid, honestly, because these aren’t complicated tasks for me. I’ve done them a million times. But lately it feels like my brain is way too easy to knock off track.\n\nDoctor: Have you had less appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of, but it’s not just less appetite exactly. It’s been really uneven, and honestly that’s part of what scared me into coming in.\n\nSome days I’ll realize it’s like mid-afternoon and I haven’t eaten anything real at all. Not because I’m trying not to eat, I just… forget, or nothing sounds good, or I’m busy and then suddenly hours are gone. And then other nights it swings the other way where it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I’m starving, just eating whatever’s there.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it just “less appetite,” because sometimes it’s like I have no appetite, and then later it’s like I’m making up for the whole day all at once. It feels out of control in a weird way.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have other people noticed that you’re moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if people would say “slower,” exactly. I haven’t had someone sit me down and say, like, “You’re moving slow” or anything.\n\nIt’s more like I feel kind of all over the place depending on where I am. At work, before appointments, I’ll catch myself pacing around my car in the parking lot instead of just going in. Like I’ll get there early, and instead of sitting still I’m walking around the car, checking my phone, tapping my hands on the steering wheel, almost like I can’t settle my body down. I probably look ridiculous if anyone sees me.\n\nBut then when I get home, it’s the opposite. I’ll sit on the couch and it feels like getting up to do one basic thing takes forever. Like I know I should stand up, or go get something, or move to the next room, but my body feels heavy and I just sit there thinking about doing it. So I don’t know if it’s that I’m moving slower all the time, but there are definitely times where I feel slowed down, and other times where I’m kind of restless and can’t stop moving.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. At first I thought it was just a bad week turning into a bad couple weeks, but it didn’t really lift. And after the trouble I had at work, it got a lot harder to ignore. That’s when it felt less like, “Oh, I’m just in a funk,” and more like, “Okay, something is actually off here.”\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel like I’m not really myself lately.\n\nI used to be pretty into my weekends, like I’d play soccer with some guys, and even if I was tired from the week I’d usually still want to go because once I got there it felt good, you know? Now I’ll see the messages about it and just… ignore them, or tell myself I’ll answer later, and then I don’t. It’s not even that I hate it now, it’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to show up.\n\nSame with cooking. I used to like making dinner for my girlfriend — not fancy chef stuff or anything, but I liked planning something, going to the store, making it feel like a nice night. Lately it feels like this huge task, and the fun part of it is just gone.\n\nEven stuff like checking out small galleries, which used to be something I’d do just because it got me out of my head, I haven’t cared about. And at work, I used to actually enjoy helping the newer sales guys figure things out, like giving them tips or walking them through how to handle certain accounts. Now when they ask me something, I feel bad because I know I should care, but I just don’t have that spark for it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It’s like… even if I technically lie down for a long time, it doesn’t feel like I really get my energy back. I’ll spend hours resting, and then the next day my body still feels drained.\n\nAnd it’s not just “I don’t feel like doing stuff.” It’s physical, too. Like at work, carrying sample cases into a client meeting will feel way harder than it should. I’ll be thinking, “Why am I this worn out from something I normally just do without thinking?” It’s embarrassing, honestly, because it makes me feel kind of weak or like I’m dragging myself through things that used to be normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my sleep’s been pretty messed up. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get in bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel tired, but then my brain just won’t shut off. I’ll start running through work stuff — pitches, what I should’ve said to a client, what I need to say next time, different ways to phrase things — and it just keeps looping. It’s like I’m rehearsing conversations I’m not even having yet.\n\nOr I’ll fall asleep for a bit, but then I wake up way before my alarm, like way too early, and I’m just… awake. Not rested, though. It’s weird because I know I barely slept, and part of me is like, “You need to go back to sleep,” but my body feels kind of wired, like I’m already switched on. So then I just lie there, thinking about the day before it’s even started. It’s been happening a lot, most nights honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like I’ve sat down and made some plan or anything like that. I haven’t done that. But I’d be lying if I said the thoughts weren’t there sometimes.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be so tired of feeling like this that I’ll think, “I wish I could just not wake up for a while.” Not even necessarily die, exactly, but just stop having to be conscious and deal with everything for a bit. Like if I could press pause on myself and not have to feel anything, that sounds… honestly kind of relieving sometimes.\n\nBut no, I haven’t planned to hurt myself. I haven’t picked a way or anything. It’s more those thoughts of not wanting to be here in the moment, or wanting everything to just go quiet.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the more frustrating things, because my job is a lot of little details, and normally I’m pretty good at keeping track of them.\n\nLike I’ll be entering an order, and I know what I’m supposed to be doing — customer name, quantities, item numbers, shipping details, all that basic stuff — but then my phone buzzes, or a truck goes by outside, or someone just walks past the office, and it completely pulls me off. Not just for a second, either. It’s like my attention gets yanked over there, and then when I look back at the screen I’ve lost the thread of what I was doing.\n\nAnd then I’ll miss things I normally wouldn’t miss. Basic details. Stuff where afterward I’m like, “How did I not catch that?” It makes me feel kind of stupid, honestly, because these aren’t complicated tasks for me. I’ve done them a million times. But lately it feels like my brain is way too easy to knock off track.\n\nDoctor: Have you had less appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of, but it’s not just less appetite exactly. It’s been really uneven, and honestly that’s part of what scared me into coming in.\n\nSome days I’ll realize it’s like mid-afternoon and I haven’t eaten anything real at all. Not because I’m trying not to eat, I just… forget, or nothing sounds good, or I’m busy and then suddenly hours are gone. And then other nights it swings the other way where it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I’m starving, just eating whatever’s there.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it just “less appetite,” because sometimes it’s like I have no appetite, and then later it’s like I’m making up for the whole day all at once. It feels out of control in a weird way.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have other people noticed that you’re moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if people would say “slower,” exactly. I haven’t had someone sit me down and say, like, “You’re moving slow” or anything.\n\nIt’s more like I feel kind of all over the place depending on where I am. At work, before appointments, I’ll catch myself pacing around my car in the parking lot instead of just going in. Like I’ll get there early, and instead of sitting still I’m walking around the car, checking my phone, tapping my hands on the steering wheel, almost like I can’t settle my body down. I probably look ridiculous if anyone sees me.\n\nBut then when I get home, it’s the opposite. I’ll sit on the couch and it feels like getting up to do one basic thing takes forever. Like I know I should stand up, or go get something, or move to the next room, but my body feels heavy and I just sit there thinking about doing it. So I don’t know if it’s that I’m moving slower all the time, but there are definitely times where I feel slowed down, and other times where I’m kind of restless and can’t stop moving.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot about different things in a way that feels hard to control?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one. I mean, I worry about things, obviously — work stuff, money stuff, whether I’m messing things up — but I don’t know if it’s “a lot” in the way you mean.\n\nIt doesn’t feel like I’m sitting there with one worry after another all day that I can’t control, exactly. Sometimes my mind gets stuck on something for a while, but I can usually tell what it’s connected to. Like if something is actually going on, I’ll think about it too much maybe, but I don’t know if I’d call it this constant uncontrollable worrying about everything.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that as the main thing. I’d probably need you to explain what you mean a little more.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I think so. Not every time, but on those nights it feels like a weird amount for that hour, if that makes sense.

Like I’ll have barely eaten during the day — maybe I forgot lunch completely or just had something small — and then after midnight I’m suddenly ordering takeout or grabbing whatever snacks are around, and I’ll keep going past the point where it seems normal. It’s not even like a planned meal. It’s more like I’m standing in the kitchen or sitting there with containers open, just eating because I suddenly feel starving or empty or something.

What brings you in?\n\nPatient: Honestly, the thing that kind of scared me into coming in is my eating. It’s been really all over the place lately. Like, some days I’ll get through almost the whole day and realize I basically didn’t eat anything, not because I’m trying to or anything, I just forget meals completely. And then other nights it’s the total opposite — I’m up late, after midnight, and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I can’t stop myself.\n\nIt’s just been weird enough that I started thinking, okay, maybe I should actually talk to somebody, because it doesn’t feel like my normal routine anymore.\n\nDoctor: When you eat like that after midnight, are you waking up from sleep to do it?\n\nPatient: Not really waking up from sleep, no. It’s more like I’m already still up way too late, and then I suddenly realize I barely ate during the day, or I just get this urge to order food or grab whatever snacks are around. Like I’ll go most of the day and completely forget meals, and then after midnight I’m eating takeout or a bunch of random stuff like I can’t slow down. It’s not like I go to bed and then wake up hungry—it’s more that my whole eating schedule is just… messed up.\n\nDoctor: When those late-night eating episodes happen, do you feel like you can’t stop eating once you’ve started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Not every single time, but when it happens it feels like once I start, I’m not really making normal decisions about it. Like I’ll order takeout after midnight or start grabbing snacks, and then it’s just this whole stretch where I’m eating whatever’s there, one thing after another. It’s not even like I’m enjoying it that much the whole time — it’s more like I suddenly realize I barely ate all day, and then I’m tearing through food like my body’s trying to catch up or something.\n\nAnd that’s what freaked me out, honestly, because it’s such a swing from forgetting meals completely during the day to then being up late and eating way more than feels normal. It doesn’t feel like my usual appetite or routine.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight or about the shape of your body?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I’m not thrilled if my clothes feel tighter or something, like anyone, but I’m not sitting there counting calories because I’m scared of gaining weight, and I’m not avoiding food because of how I think I look.\n\nIt’s more like I just genuinely forget to eat or I’m distracted, and then later it catches up with me in this messy way. The late-night eating bothers me because it feels out of control and makes me feel kind of gross afterward, but I wouldn’t say it’s about my body shape exactly. It’s more the pattern that worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. It’s hard for me to say “depressed” because I keep telling myself I’m just stressed or I’m tired or I’m being dramatic, but if I’m being honest, the low feeling has been there most days for a while now.\n\nIt’s probably been around four months, give or take. Like this heavy mood, and feeling guilty about things, and just not really caring about stuff the way I normally would. At first I thought it was just a bad week turning into a bad couple weeks, but it didn’t really lift. And after the trouble I had at work, it got a lot harder to ignore. That’s when it felt less like, “Oh, I’m just in a funk,” and more like, “Okay, something is actually off here.”\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel like I’m not really myself lately.\n\nI used to be pretty into my weekends, like I’d play soccer with some guys, and even if I was tired from the week I’d usually still want to go because once I got there it felt good, you know? Now I’ll see the messages about it and just… ignore them, or tell myself I’ll answer later, and then I don’t. It’s not even that I hate it now, it’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to show up.\n\nSame with cooking. I used to like making dinner for my girlfriend — not fancy chef stuff or anything, but I liked planning something, going to the store, making it feel like a nice night. Lately it feels like this huge task, and the fun part of it is just gone.\n\nEven stuff like checking out small galleries, which used to be something I’d do just because it got me out of my head, I haven’t cared about. And at work, I used to actually enjoy helping the newer sales guys figure things out, like giving them tips or walking them through how to handle certain accounts. Now when they ask me something, I feel bad because I know I should care, but I just don’t have that spark for it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It’s like… even if I technically lie down for a long time, it doesn’t feel like I really get my energy back. I’ll spend hours resting, and then the next day my body still feels drained.\n\nAnd it’s not just “I don’t feel like doing stuff.” It’s physical, too. Like at work, carrying sample cases into a client meeting will feel way harder than it should. I’ll be thinking, “Why am I this worn out from something I normally just do without thinking?” It’s embarrassing, honestly, because it makes me feel kind of weak or like I’m dragging myself through things that used to be normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my sleep’s been pretty messed up. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get in bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel tired, but then my brain just won’t shut off. I’ll start running through work stuff — pitches, what I should’ve said to a client, what I need to say next time, different ways to phrase things — and it just keeps looping. It’s like I’m rehearsing conversations I’m not even having yet.\n\nOr I’ll fall asleep for a bit, but then I wake up way before my alarm, like way too early, and I’m just… awake. Not rested, though. It’s weird because I know I barely slept, and part of me is like, “You need to go back to sleep,” but my body feels kind of wired, like I’m already switched on. So then I just lie there, thinking about the day before it’s even started. It’s been happening a lot, most nights honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like I’ve sat down and made some plan or anything like that. I haven’t done that. But I’d be lying if I said the thoughts weren’t there sometimes.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be so tired of feeling like this that I’ll think, “I wish I could just not wake up for a while.” Not even necessarily die, exactly, but just stop having to be conscious and deal with everything for a bit. Like if I could press pause on myself and not have to feel anything, that sounds… honestly kind of relieving sometimes.\n\nBut no, I haven’t planned to hurt myself. I haven’t picked a way or anything. It’s more those thoughts of not wanting to be here in the moment, or wanting everything to just go quiet.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the more frustrating things, because my job is a lot of little details, and normally I’m pretty good at keeping track of them.\n\nLike I’ll be entering an order, and I know what I’m supposed to be doing — customer name, quantities, item numbers, shipping details, all that basic stuff — but then my phone buzzes, or a truck goes by outside, or someone just walks past the office, and it completely pulls me off. Not just for a second, either. It’s like my attention gets yanked over there, and then when I look back at the screen I’ve lost the thread of what I was doing.\n\nAnd then I’ll miss things I normally wouldn’t miss. Basic details. Stuff where afterward I’m like, “How did I not catch that?” It makes me feel kind of stupid, honestly, because these aren’t complicated tasks for me. I’ve done them a million times. But lately it feels like my brain is way too easy to knock off track.\n\nDoctor: Have you had less appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of, but it’s not just less appetite exactly. It’s been really uneven, and honestly that’s part of what scared me into coming in.\n\nSome days I’ll realize it’s like mid-afternoon and I haven’t eaten anything real at all. Not because I’m trying not to eat, I just… forget, or nothing sounds good, or I’m busy and then suddenly hours are gone. And then other nights it swings the other way where it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I’m starving, just eating whatever’s there.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it just “less appetite,” because sometimes it’s like I have no appetite, and then later it’s like I’m making up for the whole day all at once. Not because I’m trying not to eat, I just… forget, or nothing sounds good, or I’m busy and then suddenly hours are gone. And then other nights it swings the other way where it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I’m starving, just eating whatever’s there.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it just “less appetite,” because sometimes it’s like I have no appetite, and then later it’s like I’m making up for the whole day all at once. Sometimes my mind gets stuck on something for a while, but I can usually tell what it’s connected to. The late-night eating bothers me because it feels out of control and makes me feel kind of gross afterward, but I wouldn’t say it’s about my body shape exactly. It’s more the pattern that worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. It’s hard for me to say “depressed” because I keep telling myself I’m just stressed or I’m tired or I’m being dramatic, but if I’m being honest, the low feeling has been there most days for a while now.\n\nIt’s probably been around four months, give or take. Like this heavy mood, and feeling guilty about things, and just not really caring about stuff the way I normally would. That’s when it felt less like, “Oh, I’m just in a funk,” and more like, “Okay, something is actually off here.”\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel like I’m not really myself lately.\n\nI used to be pretty into my weekends, like I’d play soccer with some guys, and even if I was tired from the week I’d usually still want to go because once I got there it felt good, you know? Now I’ll see the messages about it and just… ignore them, or tell myself I’ll answer later, and then I don’t. It’s not even that I hate it now, it’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to show up.\n\nSame with cooking. I used to like making dinner for my girlfriend — not fancy chef stuff or anything, but I liked planning something, going to the store, making it feel like a nice night. Lately it feels like this huge task, and the fun part of it is just gone.\n\nEven stuff like checking out small galleries, which used to be something I’d do just because it got me out of my head, I haven’t cared about. And at work, I used to actually enjoy helping the newer sales guys figure things out, like giving them tips or walking them through how to handle certain accounts. Now when they ask me something, I feel bad because I know I should care, but I just don’t have that spark for it. I’ll spend hours resting, and then the next day my body still feels drained.\n\nAnd it’s not just “I don’t feel like doing stuff.” It’s physical, too. Like at work, carrying sample cases into a client meeting will feel way harder than it should. I’ll be thinking, “Why am I this worn out from something I normally just do without thinking?” It’s embarrassing, honestly, because it makes me feel kind of weak or like I’m dragging myself through things that used to be normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my sleep’s been pretty messed up. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get in bed and I’m exhausted, like I feel tired, but then my brain just won’t shut off. I’ll start running through work stuff — pitches, what I should’ve said to a client, what I need to say next time, different ways to phrase things — and it just keeps looping. It’s like I’m rehearsing conversations I’m not even having yet.\n\nOr I’ll fall asleep for a bit, but then I wake up way before my alarm, like way too early, and I’m just… awake. Not rested, though. It’s weird because I know I barely slept, and part of me is like, “You need to go back to sleep,” but my body feels kind of wired, like I’m already switched on. So then I just lie there, thinking about the day before it’s even started. It’s been happening a lot, most nights honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like I’ve sat down and made some plan or anything like that. I haven’t done that. Like if I could press pause on myself and not have to feel anything, that sounds… honestly kind of relieving sometimes.\n\nBut no, I haven’t planned to hurt myself. I haven’t picked a way or anything. It’s more those thoughts of not wanting to be here in the moment, or wanting everything to just go quiet.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely.
